Since \(n\ge1\), \(x>0\), and \cref{obj:lem:zeng-usable-occupancy-reciprocal} gives
\[
\mathbb E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
\mathbf 1\{R_n>0\}\frac1{R_n}
\right]
\le
B_\epsilon\{x+\exp(-b_\epsilon/x)\},
\qquad
Q_P^{(n)}(R_n=0)\le 2\exp(-b_\epsilon/x).
\]
The elementary inequality
\[
\exp(-b_\epsilon/x)\le b_\epsilon^{-1}x,
\qquad x>0,
\]
follows from \(y\le e^y\) with \(y=b_\epsilon/x\).  Also
\[
x=\frac{n\vee d}{n^2}\le \frac1n+\frac d{n^2}.
\]
% lean: exp_neg_div_absorbed_by_linear_rate
% lean: occupancy_max_rate_le

The clipping interval contains \(\tau(P)\) by \cref{obj:lem:scale-sanity}, so clipping the inner statistic to \([-M,M]\) is a contraction of squared distance from \(\tau(P)\).  Together with \((u+v)^2\le2u^2+2v^2\), the preceding bounds give
\[
\mathbb E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[(T_n^{\mathrm{col}}-\tau(P))^2\right]
\le
2K_\epsilon B_\epsilon M^2\{x+\exp(-b_\epsilon/x)\}
+
2M^2\{\sigma^2+2\exp(-b_\epsilon/x)\}.
\]
Using the two preceding displays and the definition of \(C_\epsilon\),
\[
\mathbb E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[(T_n^{\mathrm{col}}-\tau(P))^2\right]
\le
C_\epsilon M^2
\left(\frac1n+\sigma^2+\frac d{n^2}\right),
\]
which is the asserted bound. % lean: continuous_occupancy_collision_upper_all_d
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:continuous-occupancy-collision-upper-all-d} supplies the collision branch uniformly over all alphabet sizes. The three terms have the same interpretation as in the main text: ordinary sampling variation, the radius-controlled cost of replacing uncrossed cell effects by crossed-cell information, and the sparse-cell occupancy contribution.

The polynomial branch is calibrated by choosing the degree and the active range so that the fixed-branch inequality can be summed over the pilot-induced heavy-light split. The next result packages that calibration for the total estimator in \cref{obj:def:polynomial-handle}.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:continuous-ratio-polynomial-upper-all-d}[All-alphabet polynomial upper bound]
For every overlap parameter \(0<\epsilon<1/2\), there exist a constant \(C_{\epsilon}>0\) and a polynomial handle, consisting of a cutoff \(N_{\epsilon}\in\mathbb N\) and an active-range multiplier \(\rho_{\epsilon}>0\) such that
\[
N_{\epsilon}\le n,\qquad d\le \rho_{\epsilon}n\log(en)
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
2\le K_n
\]
for all integers \(n,d\ge1\), with the following properties.
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Complexity.)} The handle admits a constant \(C_{\mathrm{op}}\in\mathbb N\), independent of \(n,d\), and \(M\), such that for every \(n,d\) and \(M\) the post-aggregation polynomial program for \(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}\) has at most
\[
C_{\mathrm{op}}(d+1)(K_n+1)^2
\]
arithmetic operations.
\item \textbf{(Total bounded estimator.)} For every pair of integers \(n,d\ge1\) and every \(M\ge1\), the estimator \(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}\) determined by this handle is a measurable function of the \(n\)-sample observed data and satisfies
\[
T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}(s)\in[-M,M]
\]
for every sample \(s\).
\item \textbf{(Risk bound.)} For every pair of integers \(n,d\ge1\), every \(M\ge1\), every \(0\le\sigma\le2\), and every \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\) as in \cref{obj:def:model-class},
\[
\mathbb E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[\{T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}-\tau(P)\}^2\right]
\le
C_{\epsilon}M^2
\left\{
\frac{1}{n}
+
\min\!\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right)
\right\}.
\]
\end{itemize}
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:continuous-ratio-polynomial-upper-all-d}]
Fix \(0<\epsilon<1/2\).  Let \(C_{\mathrm{fix}}>0\) be the constant supplied by the fixed-branch polynomial risk bound in \cref{obj:lem:polynomial-fixed-branch-risk}, after the deterministic heavy-set simplification described below.
% lean: polynomial_fixedBranch_uniform_bound

Choose integers \(N_{\deg}\) and \(N_{\log}\) such that
\[
N_{\deg}\le n \Longrightarrow 2\le K_n,
\qquad
N_{\log}\le n \Longrightarrow 240\le \log(en).
\]
These exist because \(K_n=\lfloor\alpha_0\log(en)\rfloor\) with \(\alpha_0>0\), and because \(\log(en)\to\infty\).
% lean: polynomialDegree_eventually_two
% lean: logEN_eventually_ge_240
Set
\[
N_\epsilon:=\max\{8,N_{\deg},N_{\log}\},
\qquad
\rho_\epsilon:=1 .
\]
Then \(\rho_\epsilon>0\), and if \(N_\epsilon\le n\) and \(d\le\rho_\epsilon n\log(en)\), then \(2\le K_n\).
% lean: continuous_ratio_polynomial_upper_all_d

The estimator determined by this handle is exactly the program in \cref{obj:def:polynomial-handle}, with its active branch on
\[
N_\epsilon\le n,
\qquad d\le \rho_\epsilon n\log(en),
\]
and zero fallback outside this branch.  Clipping gives, for every \(n,d\ge1\), every \(M\ge1\), and every sample \(s\),
\[
T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}(s)\in[-M,M],
\]
and the count, sum, ratio with totalized denominators, finite sums, and clipping operations are measurable functions of the sample.
% lean: polyEstimator_total_and_clipped

The arithmetic certificate is for the evaluator after one aggregation pass has formed, for each cell, the pilot count, the two estimation-block arm counts, and the two estimation-block outcome sums.  The post-aggregation syntax has constants and aggregate reads at zero cost; binary arithmetic nodes \(+, -, \times, /, \min,\max\) have cost one plus their subprogram costs; a comparison branch \(\mathbf 1\{p<q\}\) has cost one plus the two comparison-side costs and the larger branch cost; \(\sum_k\) over cells has cost \(d\) plus the body costs; \(\sum_{j<r}\) has cost \(r\) plus the term costs; the arm falling-factorial aggregate of order \(j\) has cost \(j\); and subtracting an offset from the total cell count has cost one.  With these rules, the active-branch syntax evaluates the calibrated branch in \cref{obj:def:polynomial-handle}, while a fallback branch is represented by the constant-zero syntax.

For one cell, an arm mean has cost \(2\), the heavy-cell contribution has cost \(9\), and the marked factorial term of degree index \(j\) has cost \(5+j\).  Hence each light summand has cost
\[
1+\{1+(5+j)+(5+j)\}=12+2j
\le 14(K_n+1),
\]
and the whole light-cell subtree satisfies
\[
(K_n-1)+\sum_{j=0}^{K_n-2}(12+2j)
\le 16(K_n+1)^2 .
\]
Thus the per-cell threshold branch has cost at most
\[
1+\max\{9,16(K_n+1)^2\}
\le 1+16(K_n+1)^2 .
\]
Adding the cell-sum charge and the final multiply/minimum/maximum clipping nodes gives
\[
\operatorname{opcount}
\le 3+d+d\{1+16(K_n+1)^2\}
\le 128(d+1)(K_n+1)^2
\]
for every \(n,d\), and \(M\).  Hence the public post-aggregation complexity assertion holds with \(C_{\mathrm{op}}=128\).
% lean: polynomialComplexityBound

It remains to prove the risk bound.  Put
\[
L:=\log(en),\qquad
m_1:=n-\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\qquad
B:=\frac{4096L}{m_1},
\]
and let
\[
\beta_-:=\frac{128L}{\lfloor n/2\rfloor},
\qquad
\beta_+:=\frac{512L}{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}.
\]
On the active branch and for \(n\ge N_\epsilon\), the calibration gives
\[
n\ge8,
\qquad
K_n\ge2,
\qquad
240\le L,
\]
and the deterministic inequalities
\[
\beta_+\le \frac B4,
\qquad
4(K_n+2)^2\le m_1,
\qquad
\frac{4(K_n+2)}{m_1}\le \frac{3B}{4}.
\]
% lean: polynomialPilotUpperBand_le_lightScale_quarter
% lean: polynomial_fixedBranch_size_condition
% lean: polynomial_shift_condition

For any deterministic heavy set \(H\) satisfying
\[
\beta_-\le p_k\quad(k\in H),
\qquad
p_k\le \beta_+\quad(k\notin H),
\]
\cref{obj:lem:polynomial-fixed-branch-risk}, the preceding calibration, \(\sum_{k\in H}p_k\le1\), and \(|H|,|H^{\mathrm c}|\le d\) yield
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\mathbb E_{Q_P^{(m_1)}}\!\left[\Delta_H^2\right]  \\
&\quad\le
8\left\{
\frac{8}{m_1\epsilon}
+\frac{6}{m_1}
+4\left[
\frac{d}
{\{(m_1-2)\epsilon/2\}^2\beta_-}
\right]^2
\right\} \\
&\qquad+
2\left\{
\frac{C_{\mathrm{fix}}}{m_1}
+
C_{\mathrm{fix}}6^{2K_n}
\left(dB^2+\frac{d^2K_n^2B^2}{m_1}\right)
+
\left(\frac{dB}{\epsilon K_n^2}\right)^2
\right\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Here the first braces control the heavy plug-in part and the second braces control the light polynomial part.
% lean: polynomial_fixedBranch_uniform_bound

The active-branch rate algebra is termwise.  The estimates used are
\[
\frac1{m_1}\le\frac2n,
\qquad
B\le\frac{8192L}{n},
\qquad
K_n\le L,
\qquad
\frac{\alpha_0L}{2}\le K_n,
\]
\[
4n\epsilon^2L
\le
\{(m_1-2)\epsilon/2\}^2\beta_-,
\qquad
(6^{2K_n})^2L^6\le n,
\qquad
6^{2K_n}L^6\le n.
\]
They imply the seven bounds
\[
8\frac{8}{m_1\epsilon}
\le
\frac{128}{\epsilon}
\left\{\frac1n+\frac{d^2}{n^2L^2}\right\},
\qquad
8\frac6{m_1}
\le
96
\left\{\frac1n+\frac{d^2}{n^2L^2}\right\},
\]
\[
32\left[
\frac{d}{\{(m_1-2)\epsilon/2\}^2\beta_-}
\right]^2
\le
\frac{2}{\epsilon^4}
\left\{\frac1n+\frac{d^2}{n^2L^2}\right\},
\qquad
2\frac{C_{\mathrm{fix}}}{m_1}
\le
4C_{\mathrm{fix}}
\left\{\frac1n+\frac{d^2}{n^2L^2}\right\},
\]
\[
2C_{\mathrm{fix}}6^{2K_n}dB^2
\le
2C_{\mathrm{fix}}8192^2
\left\{\frac1n+\frac{d^2}{n^2L^2}\right\},
\]
\[
2C_{\mathrm{fix}}6^{2K_n}\frac{d^2K_n^2B^2}{m_1}
\le
4C_{\mathrm{fix}}8192^2
\left\{\frac1n+\frac{d^2}{n^2L^2}\right\},
\]
and
\[
2\left(\frac{dB}{\epsilon K_n^2}\right)^2
\le
2\left(\frac{32768}{\epsilon\alpha_0^2}\right)^2
\left\{\frac1n+\frac{d^2}{n^2L^2}\right\}.
\]
Adding these inequalities gives
\[
\mathbb E_{Q_P^{(m_1)}}\!\left[\Delta_H^2\right]
\le
D_\epsilon
\left\{
\frac1n+\frac{d^2}{n^2L^2}
\right\},
\]
where
\[
D_\epsilon
=
\frac{128}{\epsilon}+96+\frac{2}{\epsilon^4}
+4C_{\mathrm{fix}}
+6C_{\mathrm{fix}}\,8192^2
+2\left(\frac{32768}{\epsilon\alpha_0^2}\right)^2 .
\]
% lean: polynomial_uniformBranch_expression_le_rate

Let \(\mathcal A\) be the pilot event
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal A:=\{s:\;&
\beta_-\le p_k(P)\text{ for every }k\in\widehat{\mathcal H}(s),\\
& p_k(P)\le\beta_+\text{ for every }k\notin\widehat{\mathcal H}(s)\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Equivalently, on \(\mathcal A\) the pilot-selected heavy set is eligible for the deterministic branch bound with lower band \(\beta_-\) and upper band \(\beta_+\).  The calibrated Chernoff bound for the pilot gives
\[
Q_P^{(n)}(\mathcal A^c)
\le
2d\exp(-32L).
\]
Under the active branch \(d\le nL\), this bad-event contribution is absorbed as
\[
8d\exp(-32L)\le \frac1n .
\]
% lean: polynomialPilotGood_compl_probability_calibrated_le
% lean: polynomial_bad_event_absorption

Conditioning on the pilot block, applying the deterministic branch bound on \(\mathcal A\), and using the clipped-error envelope \(4\) on \(\mathcal A^c\) gives
\[
\mathbb E\!\left[
\left\{
\operatorname{clip}_{[-1,1]}(Z)
-\frac{\tau(P)}{M}
\right\}^2
\right]
\le
(D_\epsilon+1)
\left\{
\frac1n+\frac{d^2}{n^2L^2}
\right\},
\]
where
\[
Z=
\sum_{k\in\widehat{\mathcal H}}\widehat h_k
+
\sum_{k\in\widehat{\mathcal L}}\widehat P_k .
\]
The target \(\tau(P)/M\) lies in \([-1,1]\) by \cref{obj:lem:scale-sanity}, so the projection step preserves this squared-loss bound and rescaling by \(M\) gives the corresponding bound for \(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}\) on the active branch.
% lean: polynomial_clipped_finiteSelector_risk
% lean: mse_polyEstimator_le_of_clipped_stream

On the active branch, \(d\le nL\) implies
\[
\frac{d^2}{n^2L^2}\le1,
\]
and hence
\[
\frac1n+\frac{d^2}{n^2L^2}
=
\frac1n+\min\left\{1,\frac{d^2}{n^2L^2}\right\}.
\]
After multiplying by \(M^2\), the active-branch risk is therefore bounded by
\[
(D_\epsilon+1)M^2
\left\{
\frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right)
\right\}.
\]
% lean: continuous_ratio_polynomial_upper_all_d

On the fallback branch, the estimator equals zero.  Since \(|\tau(P)|\le M\) by \cref{obj:lem:scale-sanity}, its risk is at most \(M^2\).  There is a constant \(C_{\mathrm{fall}}>0\), depending only on \(N_\epsilon\) and \(\rho_\epsilon\), such that
\[
M^2
\le
C_{\mathrm{fall}}M^2
\left\{
\frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right)
\right\}
\]
throughout the fallback branch: if \(n<N_\epsilon\), the \(1/n\) term is bounded below by \(1/N_\epsilon\), while if \(d>\rho_\epsilon n\log(en)\), the minimum term is bounded below by \(\min(1,\rho_\epsilon^2)\).
% lean: polyEstimator_uncalibrated_rate

Finally set
\[
C_\epsilon:=\max\{C_{\mathrm{fall}},D_\epsilon+1\}.
\]
This constant is positive, the active and fallback branches together cover all \(n,d\ge1\), and the displayed risk bound follows for every \(M\ge1\), \(0\le\sigma\le2\), and \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\).
% lean: continuous_ratio_polynomial_upper_all_d
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:continuous-ratio-polynomial-upper-all-d} turns the light-cell analysis into a usable estimator bound. The zero fallback in the inactive range keeps the estimator total for every \(n,d\), and the displayed risk bound gives the polynomial scale used by the selector in \cref{obj:def:total-estimator}. The operation count records that, after aggregation by cell and order, the implementation grows polynomially in the alphabet size and degree.

The finite-range construction theorem combines the polynomial and collision branches before the all-alphabet packaging in \cref{obj:thm:robust-upper-construction-resolution-all-d}. It keeps the calibrated active range explicit, which is useful for downstream statements that invoke the original range condition.

\begin{theoremv}{thm:robust-upper-construction-resolution}[Robust upper construction]
For every overlap parameter \(0<\epsilon<1/2\), there exist constants \(C_{\epsilon},c_{\epsilon}>0\) and a calibrated polynomial handle consisting of a cutoff \(N\) and an active-range multiplier \(\rho>0\) such that, for every \(n,d\ge 1\),
\[
N\le n,\qquad d\le \rho n\log(en)
\]
implies that the associated polynomial degree is at least \(2\). For every \(n,d\ge 1\), \(M\ge 1\), and \(0\le \sigma\le 2\), the handle-indexed polynomial estimator \(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}\) and the occupancy-weighted estimator \(T_n^{\mathrm{col}}\) of \cref{obj:def:collision-handle} are measurable functions of the sample and take values in \([-M,M]\).

If, in addition,
\[
d\le c_{\epsilon}\frac{n^2}{\log(en)},
\]
then for every \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\) as defined in \cref{obj:def:model-class},
\[
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[\left(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}-\tau(P)\right)^2\right]
\le
C_{\epsilon}M^2
\left\{
\frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right)
\right\},
\]
and
\[
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[\left(T_n^{\mathrm{col}}-\tau(P)\right)^2\right]
\le
C_{\epsilon}M^2
\left(
\frac1n+\sigma^2+\frac{d}{n^2}
\right).
\]
\end{theoremv}

\Cref{obj:thm:robust-upper-construction-resolution} states the two estimators on the same calibrated range. The polynomial branch contributes the large-alphabet approximation rate, and the occupancy branch contributes the radius-sensitive collision rate. Since both estimators are clipped into \([-M,M]\), the selector can compare their deterministic benchmark terms within the admissible class of \cref{obj:def:minimax-risk}.

The final result in this appendix is the restricted selector bound. It is the finite-range version of the selector statement used in the main text and follows by applying the branch with the smaller displayed nonparametric benchmark.

\begin{theoremv}{thm:frontier-upper}[Restricted frontier upper bound]
For every overlap parameter \(\epsilon\) with \(0<\epsilon<1/2\), there exist constants \(C_{\epsilon},c_{\epsilon}>0\) and a polynomial calibration handle \((N,\rho)\) with \(\rho>0\) such that, for all positive integers \(n,d\),
\[
N\le n
\quad\text{and}\quad
d\le \rho n\log(en)
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
2\le K_n ,
\]
where \(K_n\) is the active polynomial degree in the polynomial estimator.  For every \(n,d,M,\sigma\), assume:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Sample and alphabet.)} \(n\ge1\) and \(d\ge1\).
\item \textbf{(Envelope and radius.)} \(M\ge1\) and \(0\le\sigma\le2\).
\item \textbf{(Restricted dimension.)}
\[
d\le c_{\epsilon}\frac{n^2}{\log(en)} .
\]
\end{itemize}
Let \(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}\in\mathcal T_{n,M}\) be the polynomial estimator determined by this handle, let \(T_n^{\mathrm{col}}\in\mathcal T_{n,M}\) be the collision estimator, and let \(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}\) be the known-radius selector of \cref{obj:def:total-estimator} applied to \(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}\), \(T_n^{\mathrm{col}}\), and \(\sigma\).  Put
\[
r_{n,d,\sigma}
=
\frac1n+
\min\left\{
1,\,
\min\left\{
\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)},\,
\sigma^2+\frac{d}{n^2}
\right\}
\right\}.
\]
Then \(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}\in\mathcal T_{n,M}\), and for every \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\) in \cref{obj:def:model-class},
\[
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
\left(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}-\tau(P)\right)^2
\right]
\le
C_{\epsilon}M^2 r_{n,d,\sigma}.
\]
\end{theoremv}

\Cref{obj:thm:frontier-upper} records the finite-range selector guarantee behind the all-alphabet statement in \cref{obj:thm:frontier-upper-all-d}. The benchmark \(r_{n,d,\sigma}\) combines the parametric term with the smallest of the clipped-zero, polynomial, and collision remainders. This is the constructive upper-bound input used when the main bracket is assembled in \cref{obj:thm:two-sided-minimax-bracket-all-d}.


\section{Proofs for lower bounds and regime algebra}

This appendix records the auxiliary lower-bound and algebraic statements that support the main bracket. The proof has four mechanisms. The parametric lower bound supplies the \(M^2/n\) sampling term. The exact-homogeneity transport supplies the capped collision baseline \(M^2\{n^{-1}+\min(1,d/n^2)\}\). The radius-channel transport supplies the rare-cell term \(M^2\sigma^2\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}\). The final rate algebra compares these lower benchmarks with the selector benchmark and identifies the matched regimes and residual wedge.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:parametric-lower}[Parametric minimax lower bound]
For every overlap parameter \(\epsilon\) satisfying \(0<\epsilon<1/2\), there exists a constant \(c_{\epsilon}>0\) such that the following holds. For every \(n,d\in\mathbb N\) and every \(M,\sigma\in\mathbb R\), suppose that
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Sample and alphabet sizes.)} \(n\ge 1\) and \(d\ge 1\);
\item \textbf{(Outcome radius.)} \(M\ge 1\);
\item \textbf{(Noise radius.)} \(0\le \sigma\le 2\).
\end{itemize}
Then the minimax mean-squared risk \(\mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\) of \cref{obj:def:minimax-risk} satisfies
\[
\mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\ge \frac{c_{\epsilon}M^2}{n}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:parametric-lower}]
Take
\[
c_\epsilon:=\frac1{100}.
\]
% lean: parametric_lower_core
Fix \(n,d,M,\sigma\) satisfying the displayed hypotheses, and choose a cell \(k\in[d]\).  Put
\[
\delta:=\frac{2/5}{\sqrt n},
\qquad
u_v:=M(1/2+v\delta-1/2)\quad (v\in\{0,1\}).
\]
Thus \(u_0=0\) and \(u_1=M\delta\).  Moreover,
\[
\delta^2=\frac{4}{25n},
\qquad
0\le\delta\le\frac25,
\qquad
|u_0|\le M/2,
\qquad
|u_1|\le M/2 .
\]
% lean: parametric_lower_core
For \(v\in\{0,1\}\), let \(P_v\) be the one-cell test law with \(X=k\), \(P(A=1\mid X=k)=1/2\), control potential outcome \(Y(0)=0\), and treated potential outcome \(Y(1)\) distributed on \(\{-M/2,M/2\}\) with mean \(u_v\).  Equivalently, draw \(A\) with probability \(1/2\) independently of this treated endpoint and set \(Y=Y(A)\).  Thus, for every cell \(\ell\), arm \(a\), and measurable \(S_0,S_1\subseteq\mathbb R\),
\[
P_v\{X=\ell,A=a,Y(0)\in S_0,Y(1)\in S_1\}\,P_v\{X=\ell\}
=
P_v\{X=\ell,A=a\}\,P_v\{X=\ell,Y(0)\in S_0,Y(1)\in S_1\},
\]
so the conditional exchangeability requirement in \cref{obj:def:model-class} holds, and consistency holds from \(Y=Y(A)\).  The same construction has cell mass one at \(k\), overlap \(1/2\), conditional means \(0\) and \(u_v\), second central moments bounded by \((M/2)^2\le M^2\), and cell deviation \(0\).  Together with \(0<\epsilon<1/2\), \(M\ge1\), and \(0\le\sigma\le2\), this gives
\[
P_v\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}
\qquad (v=0,1).
\]
% lean: testModelClass
The targets are
\[
\tau(P_0)=0,
\qquad
\tau(P_1)=M\delta .
\]
% lean: testRealLaw_rawAte

Introduce the binary one-cell endpoint pair used for the comparison, and write \(\widetilde Q_v^{(m)}\) for its \(m\)-fold binary observed law under \(v\in\{0,1\}\).  Both source laws have the unique cell, propensity \(m_0=1/2\), and control endpoint probability \(g_0=1/2\).  The null source law has treated endpoint probability \(g_1=1/2\), while the perturbed source law has treated endpoint probability \(g_1+\delta=1/2+\delta\); this is admissible because \(0\le\delta\) and \(1/2+\delta\le1\).  The one-observation chi-square calculation gives
\[
\chi^2\!\left(\widetilde Q_1^{(1)},\widetilde Q_0^{(1)}\right)
=
\frac{(1/2)\delta^2}{(1/2)(1-1/2)}.
\]
The null one-observation atoms are positive, and tensorization gives
\[
1+
\chi^2\!\left(\widetilde Q_1^{(n)},\widetilde Q_0^{(n)}\right)
=
\left(1+\frac{(1/2)\delta^2}{(1/2)(1-1/2)}\right)^n
\le
\exp\!\left(
 n\frac{(1/2)\delta^2}{(1/2)(1-1/2)}
\right).
\]
Since
\[
n\,
\frac{(1/2)\delta^2}{(1/2)(1-1/2)}
=
\frac{8}{25}
\le \log 2,
\]
we have \(\chi^2(\widetilde Q_1^{(n)},\widetilde Q_0^{(n)})\le1\).  The chi-square-to-total-variation inequality and symmetry of total variation therefore yield
\[
\operatorname{TV}\!\left(\widetilde Q_0^{(n)},\widetilde Q_1^{(n)}\right)
\le
\frac12\sqrt{\chi^2\!\left(\widetilde Q_1^{(n)},\widetilde Q_0^{(n)}\right)}
\le\frac12 .
\]
The two real observed \(n\)-sample laws are the corresponding images under the coordinatewise affine map
\[
(x,a,b)\longmapsto (k,a,\;{\bf 1}\{a=1\}M(b-1/2)).
\]
% lean: test_product_map_null
% lean: test_product_map_pert
Therefore, by data processing for total variation,
\[
\operatorname{TV}\!\left(Q_{P_0}^{(n)},Q_{P_1}^{(n)}\right)\le\frac12 .
\]
% lean: parametric_lower_core

For any measurable estimator \(T\in\mathcal T_{n,M}\), the two-point testing inequality gives
\[
\frac{(M\delta)^2}{16}
\le
\max_{v\in\{0,1\}}
E_{Q_{P_v}^{(n)}}\!\left[(T-\tau(P_v))^2\right].
\]
% lean: test_minimax_two_point
The supremum over \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\) is finite because \(T\in[-M,M]\) and \(|\tau(P)|\le M\) by \cref{obj:lem:scale-sanity}, so the preceding bound passes through the estimator infimum in \cref{obj:def:minimax-risk}. % lean: test_model_mse_le
Using \(\delta^2=4/(25n)\),
\[
\mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}
\ge
\frac{M^2\delta^2}{16}
=
\frac{M^2}{100n}
=
\frac{c_\epsilon M^2}{n}.
\]
% lean: parametric_lower_core
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:parametric-lower} anchors the lower benchmark at the scale of estimating a bounded real mean from \(n\) observations. This component is combined below with the exact-homogeneity collision scale and the radius-channel rare-cell scale.

The radius-sensitive converse is organized through a channel from binary source experiments into the real-outcome class. The channel preserves the cell and treatment-assignment structure, contracts binary response contrasts by \(\sigma/2\), and then rescales by \(M\).

\begin{definitionv}{synth_5}[Radius-channel hard-family embedding]
For \(0\le\sigma\le2\), set \(\lambda:=\sigma/2\). The embedding \(\Psi\) sends a binary source law from the radius-channel hard family, after padding unused cells with zero mass in the \(d\)-alphabet, to the real-outcome law obtained by the hypothesis-independent channel
\[
B(a)\longmapsto B'(a)\sim\operatorname{Bernoulli}\!\left\{\frac12+\lambda\left(B(a)-\frac12\right)\right\},
\qquad
Y(a):=M\{B'(a)-1/2\},
\qquad
Y=Y(A).
\]
For source laws \(P_0\) and \(P_1\) in the realized family, the embedded targets satisfy
\[
\tau(\Psi(P_1))-\tau(\Psi(P_0))
=
M\frac{\sigma}{2}\{\psi(P_1)-\psi(P_0)\}.
\]
\end{definitionv}

\Cref{obj:synth_5} is the finite-sample transport map used in the radius-channel lower bound. The target identity shows that a binary separation for \(\psi\) becomes an average-treatment-effect separation with scale \(M\sigma\) in the embedded real-outcome experiment.

The next two transport lemmas calibrate the capped alphabet sizes for the exact-homogeneity and radius-channel components. Their role is purely quantitative: they convert the published binary lower-risk levels into the normalized expressions used by the finite-range bracket.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:capped-exact-transport-package}[Capped exact transport scale]
For every overlap level \(0<\epsilon<1/2\), there exist constants
\(b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}},c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}>0\) and a cutoff
\(N_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}\in\mathbb N\) such that the following properties hold.
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Capped alphabet.)} For every pair of integers \(n,d\ge1\), define the exact capped alphabet size from \cref{obj:def:least-favorable-handle} by
\[
d_{\mathrm{ex}}
=
\min\left\{d,\max\left\{1,\left\lfloor b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}n^2\right\rfloor\right\}\right\}.
\]
Then \(0<d_{\mathrm{ex}}\le d\), and whenever \(N_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}\le n\),
\[
d_{\mathrm{ex}}\le b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}n^2 .
\]

\item \textbf{(Estimator-wise source risk.)} For every \(n,d\ge1\), with \(d_{\mathrm{ex}}\) defined above, there is a real number \(L_{\mathrm{ex}}=L_{\mathrm{ex}}(n,d)\) such that every measurable estimator
\[
\widehat\psi:\bigl(\{1,\ldots,n\}\to \bigl(\{1,\ldots,d_{\mathrm{ex}}\}\times\{0,1\}\times\{0,1\}\bigr)\bigr)\to\mathbb R
\]
admits an exactly homogeneous binary law \(R\) over \(d_{\mathrm{ex}}\) cells at overlap \(\epsilon\) for which
\[
L_{\mathrm{ex}}
\le
\mathbb E_{Q_R^{(n)}}\!\left[
\{\widehat\psi-\psi(R)\}^2
\right].
\]

\item \textbf{(Transport normalization.)} For every \(n,d\ge1\), with the same \(L_{\mathrm{ex}}(n,d)\), and for every \(M\in\mathbb R\),
\[
c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}M^2
\left\{\frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac d{n^2}\right)\right\}
\le
M^2L_{\mathrm{ex}} .
\]
\end{itemize}
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:capped-exact-transport-package}]
Fix \(\epsilon\in(0,1/2)\).  Let \(a_\epsilon,b_\epsilon>0\) and \(N_\epsilon\in\mathbb N\) be the constants in \cref{obj:lem:zeng-binary-exact-homogeneity-lower}.  Set
\[
b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}:=\min\{b_\epsilon,1/2\},
\qquad
K_\epsilon:=1+N_\epsilon+\frac1{b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}},
\]
\[
c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\min\left\{\frac{a_\epsilon b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}}{4},
\frac{1}{200K_\epsilon}\right\},
\qquad
N_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\max\left\{N_\epsilon,\left\lceil\frac1{b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}}\right\rceil\right\}.
\]
The two real constants have the asserted signs, and \(N_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}\) is a natural cutoff. % lean: exists_capped_exact_transport_package

For \(n,d\ge1\), put
\[
x:=b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}n^2,
\qquad
d_{\mathrm{ex}}
:=
\min\{d,\max\{1,\lfloor x\rfloor\}\}.
\]
Since \(d\ge1\), immediately
\[
0<d_{\mathrm{ex}}\le d .
\]
If \(N_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}\le n\), then \(1/b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}\le n\), so \(b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}n\ge1\).  Together with \(n\ge1\), this gives \(1\le x\), and therefore
\[
d_{\mathrm{ex}}
\le
\max\{1,\lfloor x\rfloor\}
=
\lfloor x\rfloor
\le x
=
b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}n^2 .
\]
% lean: capped_positive_mass_alphabet_bounds
% lean: exists_capped_exact_transport_package

It remains to construct \(L_{\mathrm{ex}}\).  First suppose
\[
N_\epsilon\le n
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
1\le x .
\]
Then
\[
d_{\mathrm{ex}}=\min\{d,\lfloor x\rfloor\}
\quad\text{and}\quad
d_{\mathrm{ex}}\le b_\epsilon n^2,
\]
because \(b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}\le b_\epsilon\).  Define
\[
s_{\mathrm{ex}}:=\frac1n+\frac{d_{\mathrm{ex}}}{n^2},
\qquad
L_{\mathrm{ex}}:=\frac{a_\epsilon}{2}s_{\mathrm{ex}} .
\]
The quantity \(s_{\mathrm{ex}}\) is positive.  Hence \(L_{\mathrm{ex}}\) lies strictly below
\(a_\epsilon s_{\mathrm{ex}}\), and \cref{obj:lem:zeng-binary-exact-homogeneity-lower} gives
\[
a_\epsilon s_{\mathrm{ex}}
\le
\inf_{\widehat\psi}
\sup_{R\in\mathcal H_{n,d_{\mathrm{ex}}}^{\mathrm{bin}}(\epsilon)}
\mathbb E_{Q_R^{(n)}}\!\left[
\{\widehat\psi-\psi(R)\}^2
\right].
\]
Since \(L_{\mathrm{ex}}\) is strictly below the displayed minimax lower bound, the definition of the infimum and supremum implies that every measurable estimator has some exactly homogeneous binary source law \(R\) over \(d_{\mathrm{ex}}\) cells such that
\[
L_{\mathrm{ex}}
\le
\mathbb E_{Q_R^{(n)}}\!\left[
\{\widehat\psi-\psi(R)\}^2
\right].
\]
% lean: exact_source_risk_of_binaryExact_lower

In this large-source case,
\[
\frac{b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}}{2}
\left\{\frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac d{n^2}\right)\right\}
\le
\frac1n+\frac{d_{\mathrm{ex}}}{n^2}.
\]
If \(d\le\lfloor x\rfloor\), then \(d_{\mathrm{ex}}=d\) and \(d/n^2\le1\).  If \(d>\lfloor x\rfloor\), then \(d_{\mathrm{ex}}=\lfloor x\rfloor\) and \(\lfloor x\rfloor\ge x/2\), so \(d_{\mathrm{ex}}/n^2\ge b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}/2\); together with \(b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}/2\le1\), this absorbs both terms on the left. % lean: half_le_natFloor_of_one_le

Since \(c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}\le a_\epsilon b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}/4\), the preceding display gives, for every \(M\in\mathbb R\),
\[
c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}M^2
\left\{\frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac d{n^2}\right)\right\}
\le
M^2L_{\mathrm{ex}} .
\]
% lean: exists_capped_exact_transport_package

Now consider the complementary case: either \(n<N_\epsilon\) or \(x<1\).  Define
\[
L_{\mathrm{ex}}:=\frac1{200n}.
\]
Since \(d_{\mathrm{ex}}>0\), the exact binary source class over \(d_{\mathrm{ex}}\) cells contains the following two uniform-mass endpoint laws.  In both laws the propensity is \(1/2\) in every cell and the control-arm binary mean is \(1/2\) in every cell.  Under \(R_0\), the treated-arm binary mean is \(1/2\); under \(R_1\), it is \(1/2+\delta\), where
\[
\delta:=\frac{2}{5\sqrt n},
\qquad
\delta^2=\frac{4}{25n}.
\]
The inequality \(n\ge1\) gives \(0<\delta\le2/5\), so both endpoint laws are valid binary laws with overlap \(\epsilon\) and a common cell effect.  Their source targets satisfy
\[
\psi(R_0)=0,
\qquad
\psi(R_1)=\delta .
\]
Moreover
\[
n\,\frac{(1/2)\delta^2}{(1/2)(1-1/2)}
=
\frac{8}{25}
\le \log 2,
\]
so the Bernoulli-product total-variation bound gives
\[
\operatorname{TV}\!\left(Q_{R_0}^{(n)},Q_{R_1}^{(n)}\right)\le\frac12 .
\]
The two-point testing bound therefore gives the exact-source minimax inequality
\[
\frac1{100n}
=
\frac{\delta^2}{16}
\le
\inf_{\widehat\psi}
\sup_{R\in\mathcal H_{n,d_{\mathrm{ex}}}^{\mathrm{bin}}(\epsilon)}
\mathbb E_{Q_R^{(n)}}\!\left[
\{\widehat\psi-\psi(R)\}^2
\right].
\]
Because \(1/(200n)<1/(100n)\), the same infimum-supremum argument yields: every measurable estimator has some exactly homogeneous binary source law \(R\) over \(d_{\mathrm{ex}}\) cells satisfying
\[
L_{\mathrm{ex}}
\le
\mathbb E_{Q_R^{(n)}}\!\left[
\{\widehat\psi-\psi(R)\}^2
\right].
\]
% lean: exact_source_risk_of_parametric_lower

In this fallback case,
\[
\frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac d{n^2}\right)
\le
\frac{K_\epsilon}{n}.
\]
Indeed, if \(n<N_\epsilon\), then \(N_\epsilon/n\ge1\); if \(x<1\), then \(1/(b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}n)\ge1\).  In either case the right side
\[
\frac{K_\epsilon}{n}
=
\frac1n+\frac{N_\epsilon}{n}
+\frac1{b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}n}
\]
dominates \(n^{-1}+1\), and the displayed bound follows. % lean: exists_capped_exact_transport_package

Because \(c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}\le 1/(200K_\epsilon)\), this gives
\[
c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}M^2
\left\{\frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac d{n^2}\right)\right\}
\le
M^2\frac1{200n}
=
M^2L_{\mathrm{ex}} .
\]
The two cases supply the same capped alphabet properties, the estimator-wise source risk, and the transport normalization for all \(n,d\ge1\). % lean: exists_capped_exact_transport_package
\end{proof}

The exact transport package expresses the binary exactly homogeneous collision lower bound at the scale of the real-outcome target. The capped alphabet \(d_{\mathrm{ex}}\) keeps the embedded source within the available cells, while the normalization clause yields the baseline \(M^2\{n^{-1}+\min(1,d/n^2)\}\).

\begin{lemmav}{lem:capped-radial-transport-scale}[Capped radial transport scale]
Let \(n,d\) be integers and let \(a,b,M,\sigma\in\mathbb R\).  Suppose that
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Sample size.)} \(n\ge 3\).
\item \textbf{(Alphabet size.)} \(d\ge 1\).
\item \textbf{(Scale.)} \(a\ge 0\).
\item \textbf{(Radial cap.)} \(0<b\le 4\).
\item \textbf{(Range.)} \(1\le (b/2)n\log(en)\).
\end{itemize}
Define the capped radial alphabet size from \cref{obj:def:least-favorable-handle} by
\[
d_{\mathrm{rad}}
:=
\min\left\{
d,\,
\max\left\{1,\left\lfloor (b/2)n\log(en)\right\rfloor\right\}
\right\}.
\]
Then
\[
\frac{ab^2}{128}M^2\sigma^2
\min\left\{1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right\}
\le
\left(\frac{M\sigma}{2}\right)^2
\frac a2
\left\{
\frac1n+
\frac{d_{\mathrm{rad}}^2}{n^2\log^2 n}
\right\}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:capped-radial-transport-scale}]
Let
\[
L:=\log(en),
\qquad
x:=\frac b2\,nL,
\qquad
d_{\mathrm{rad}}
=
\min\left\{d,\max\{1,\lfloor x\rfloor\}\right\}.
\]
Since \(n\ge3\), both \(\log n\) and \(L\) are positive, \(L\ge\log n\), and the denominator \(nL\) is positive. The range assumption gives \(1\le x\), hence \(\lfloor x\rfloor>0\) and \(\max\{1,\lfloor x\rfloor\}=\lfloor x\rfloor\). % lean: cappedRadial_sourceRate_dominates

First,
\[
\frac b4\min\left\{1,\frac d{nL}\right\}
\le
\frac{d_{\mathrm{rad}}}{nL}.
\]
If \(d\le\lfloor x\rfloor\), then \(d_{\mathrm{rad}}=d\). The fraction \(d/(nL)\) is nonnegative, so \(\min\{1,d/(nL)\}\) is nonnegative and at most \(d/(nL)\). Together with \(b/4\le1\), this gives
\[
\frac b4\min\left\{1,\frac d{nL}\right\}
\le
\min\left\{1,\frac d{nL}\right\}
\le
\frac d{nL}
=
\frac{d_{\mathrm{rad}}}{nL}.
\]
If \(d>\lfloor x\rfloor\), then \(d_{\mathrm{rad}}=\lfloor x\rfloor\). Since \(x\ge1\), \(\lfloor x\rfloor\ge x/2\), and positivity of \(nL\) gives
\[
\frac{d_{\mathrm{rad}}}{nL}
\ge
\frac{x/2}{nL}
=
\frac b4.
\]
Also \(\min\{1,d/(nL)\}\le1\) and \(b/4\ge0\), so the desired inequality follows in this case as well. % lean: cappedRadial_sourceRate_dominates

Both sides of the preceding inequality are nonnegative, hence squaring gives
\[
\left(
\frac b4\min\left\{1,\frac d{nL}\right\}
\right)^2
\le
\left(\frac{d_{\mathrm{rad}}}{nL}\right)^2.
\]
It remains to rewrite the squared minimum. Since \(d/(nL)\ge0\),
\[
\left(\min\left\{1,\frac d{nL}\right\}\right)^2
=
\min\left\{1,\frac{d^2}{n^2L^2}\right\}.
\]
Indeed, if \(d/(nL)\le1\), then the left minimum is \(d/(nL)\), and nonnegativity gives \((d/(nL))^2\le1\). If \(1\le d/(nL)\), then the left minimum is \(1\), and nonnegativity gives \(1\le(d/(nL))^2\). Using also \((d/(nL))^2=d^2/(n^2L^2)\), the squared inequality becomes
\[
\frac{b^2}{16}
\min\left\{1,\frac{d^2}{n^2L^2}\right\}
\le
\frac{d_{\mathrm{rad}}^2}{n^2L^2}.
\]
Since \(L\ge\log n>0\),
\[
\frac{d_{\mathrm{rad}}^2}{n^2L^2}
\le
\frac{d_{\mathrm{rad}}^2}{n^2\log^2 n}.
\]
Adding the nonnegative term \(1/n\) on the right therefore yields
\[
\frac{b^2}{16}
\min\left\{1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right\}
\le
\frac1n+
\frac{d_{\mathrm{rad}}^2}{n^2\log^2 n}.
\]
% lean: cappedRadial_sourceRate_dominates

Multiplying this inequality by the nonnegative factor
\[
\left(\frac{M\sigma}{2}\right)^2\frac a2
\]
and simplifying constants yields
\[
\frac{ab^2}{128}M^2\sigma^2
\min\left\{1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right\}
\le
\left(\frac{M\sigma}{2}\right)^2
\frac a2
\left\{
\frac1n+
\frac{d_{\mathrm{rad}}^2}{n^2\log^2 n}
\right\}.
\]
% lean: cappedRadial_transportScale
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:capped-radial-transport-scale} translates the source lower-risk scale into the rare-cell expression \(M^2\sigma^2\min\{1,d^2/(n^2\log^2(en))\}\). The capped \(d_{\mathrm{rad}}\) matches the radius-channel construction in \cref{obj:def:least-favorable-handle}.

Small sample sizes are absorbed by a separate finite-sample comparison. This keeps the radius-channel lower bound calibrated uniformly over the range in which the asymptotic radial cap is inactive.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:finite-sample-radial-transport-scale}[Finite-sample radial scale]
Let \(n,d,N\in\mathbb N\) satisfy \(1\le n\le N\), and let \(M,\sigma\in\mathbb R\).  Define
\[
u_{n,d}=\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}.
\]
Then
\[
\frac{1}{800N}\,M^2\sigma^2\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}
\le
\left(\frac{M\sigma}{2}\right)^2\frac{1}{200n}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:finite-sample-radial-transport-scale}]
Put
\[
u_{n,d}=\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)} .
\]
The minimum is bounded by its first entry, so
\[
\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}\le 1 .
\]
The assumptions \(1\le n\le N\) imply \(N>0\).  Hence
\[
0\le \frac{1}{800N}M^2\sigma^2,
\]
since the remaining factors are squares.  Multiplying the preceding minimum bound by this nonnegative factor gives
\[
\frac{1}{800N}M^2\sigma^2\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}
\le
\frac{1}{800N}M^2\sigma^2 .
\]
% lean: finiteSampleRadial_transportScale
The same assumptions give
\[
\frac1N\le \frac1n .
\]
Multiplying this reciprocal bound by the nonnegative factors \(1/800\), \(M^2\), and \(\sigma^2\) yields
\[
\frac{1}{800N}M^2\sigma^2
\le
\frac{1}{800n}M^2\sigma^2 .
\]
% lean: finiteSampleRadial_transportScale
Finally,
\[
\frac{1}{800n}M^2\sigma^2
=
\left(\frac{M\sigma}{2}\right)^2\frac{1}{200n},
\]
which proves the claim.
% lean: finiteSampleRadial_transportScale
\end{proof}

Together, \cref{obj:lem:capped-radial-transport-scale,obj:lem:finite-sample-radial-transport-scale} supply the scale algebra behind the finite-range radius-channel converse. The converse itself combines the transported radial component with the parametric and exact-homogeneity components.

\begin{theoremv}{thm:radius-channel-converse}[Radius-channel converse]
For every overlap parameter \(\epsilon\) satisfying \(0<\epsilon<1/2\), there exist constants \(c_{\epsilon}\) and \(b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}\) such that
\[
0<c_{\epsilon}\le 1,
\qquad
0<b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}.
\]
For every \(n,d\in\mathbb N\), \(M\in\mathbb R\), and \(\sigma\in\mathbb R\), suppose that
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Sample and alphabet.)} \(0<n\) and \(0<d\).
\item \textbf{(Scale and radius.)} \(1\le M\) and \(0\le\sigma\le2\).
\item \textbf{(Restricted range.)}
\[
d\le c_{\epsilon}\frac{n^2}{\log(en)}.
\]
\end{itemize}
Then the minimax risk from \cref{obj:def:minimax-risk} satisfies
\[
\mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}
\ge
c_{\epsilon}M^2
\left\{
\frac1n+\frac d{n^2}
+\sigma^2
\min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right)
\right\}.
\]
Moreover, the radius-channel hard family can be realized with the same constants: there are an integer cap, a source class, a padding map into the \(d\)-alphabet, an embedding \(\Psi\) of the source laws into real-outcome laws, and a coupling for the binary full-observation experiment such that the realized radial-channel data have radius parameter \(b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}\), and for all source laws \(P_0\) and \(P_1\),
\[
\tau(\Psi(P_1))-\tau(\Psi(P_0))
=
M\frac{\sigma}{2}
\left\{\psi(P_1)-\psi(P_0)\right\}.
\]
For every total measurable \([-M,M]\)-valued estimator \(\widehat\tau\), some source law \(P\) in this realized family satisfies
\[
c_{\epsilon}M^2\sigma^2
\min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right)
\le
\mathbb E_{\Psi(P)}
\left[
\left\{\widehat\tau-\tau(\Psi(P))\right\}^2
\right].
\]
\end{theoremv}

\Cref{obj:thm:radius-channel-converse} establishes the finite-range lower benchmark used in the original calibrated bracket. Its first two terms give the parametric and collision baseline, and the final term is the radius-channel contribution. The displayed source-target identity records the mechanism of the transport: source contrasts in \(\psi\) become real-outcome contrasts in \(\tau\) through the attenuation \(\sigma/2\) and the outcome scale \(M\), as in classical two-point and fuzzy-hypothesis lower-bound arguments \citep{LeCam1986,Tsybakov2009,Cai2011,Donoho1990}.

Combining the finite-range converse with the constructive selector upper bound gives the restricted-range bracket. This statement is the calibrated predecessor of the all-alphabet bracket in \cref{obj:thm:two-sided-minimax-bracket-all-d}.

\begin{theoremv}{thm:two-sided-minimax-bracket}[Two-sided minimax bracket]
For every overlap parameter \(\epsilon\) with \(0<\epsilon<1/2\), there exist real constants \(c_{\epsilon}\) and \(C_{\epsilon}\) such that
\[
0<c_{\epsilon}\le 1,\qquad 1\le C_{\epsilon},\qquad c_{\epsilon}\le C_{\epsilon}.
\]
For every pair of positive integers \(n,d\) and every \(M,\sigma\in\mathbb R\), assume:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Scale.)} \(1\le M\).
\item \textbf{(Radius.)} \(0\le \sigma\le 2\).
\item \textbf{(Range.)}
\[
d\le \frac{c_{\epsilon}n^2}{\log(en)}.
\]
\end{itemize}
Let \(\mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\) be the minimax mean-squared risk in \cref{obj:def:minimax-risk}. Then
\[
c_{\epsilon}M^2\left\{
\frac{1}{n}+\frac{d}{n^2}
+\sigma^2\min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right)
\right\}
\le
\mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}
\le
C_{\epsilon}M^2\left\{
\frac{1}{n}
+\min\left(1,\min\left(\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)},\,\sigma^2+\frac{d}{n^2}\right)\right)
\right\}.
\]
\end{theoremv}

\Cref{obj:thm:two-sided-minimax-bracket} places the finite-range upper and lower benchmarks on the same model class and loss scale. The upper expression is the selector rate, while the lower expression adds the exact-homogeneity baseline to the radius-sensitive rare-cell component. This form is useful for comparisons that keep \(d\) below the displayed calibrated range.

The endpoint reductions identify the two boundary cases of the finite-range bracket. Exact homogeneity reduces the selector and converse expressions to the collision rate, while radius two aligns the radius-indexed class with the unrestricted-radius class.

\begin{propositionv}{prop:endpoint-reductions}[Endpoint rate reductions]
For every overlap parameter \(\epsilon\in(0,1/2)\), there exist constants \(c_\epsilon,c,C\in\mathbb R\) such that
\[
0<c_\epsilon\le 1,
\qquad
0<c\le C .
\]
Write
\[
L_n=\log(en),
\qquad
u_{n,d}=\frac{d^2}{n^2L_n^2}.
\]
Then the following statements hold.
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Exact-homogeneity endpoint.)} For all integers \(n,d\ge1\) such that
\[
d\le c_\epsilon\,\frac{n^2}{L_n},
\]
the upper-frontier expression
\[
\frac1n+\min\left\{1,\min\left\{u_{n,d},\frac d{n^2}\right\}\right\}
\]
and the capped converse expression
\[
\frac1n+\min\left\{1,\frac d{n^2}\right\}
\]
are both comparable to \(n^{-1}+d/n^2\):
\[
c\left(\frac1n+\frac d{n^2}\right)
\le
\frac1n+\min\left\{1,\min\left\{u_{n,d},\frac d{n^2}\right\}\right\}
\le
C\left(\frac1n+\frac d{n^2}\right),
\]
and
\[
c\left(\frac1n+\frac d{n^2}\right)
\le
\frac1n+\min\left\{1,\frac d{n^2}\right\}
\le
C\left(\frac1n+\frac d{n^2}\right).
\]
\item \textbf{(Radius-two class identity.)} For every integer \(d\) and every \(M\in\mathbb R\), the radius-two class in \cref{obj:def:model-class} and the unrestricted class in \cref{obj:def:unrestricted-class} have exactly the same attainable laws: every law in \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M}^{\mathrm{unr}}\) is the law of some member of \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,2}\), and every law in \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,2}\) is the law of some member of \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M}^{\mathrm{unr}}\).
\item \textbf{(Radius-two endpoint.)} For all integers \(n,d\ge1\), the upper-frontier expression at radius two is exactly
\[
\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\},
\]
and the capped converse expression at radius two,
\[
\frac1n+\min\left\{1,\frac d{n^2}\right\}+4\min\{1,u_{n,d}\},
\]
is comparable to the same rate:
\[
c\left(\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}\right)
\le
\frac1n+\min\left\{1,\frac d{n^2}\right\}+4\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}
\le
C\left(\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}\right).
\]
\end{itemize}
\end{propositionv}

\Cref{obj:prop:endpoint-reductions} gives the finite-range endpoint algebra behind the rate discussion. At \(\sigma=0\), the polynomial branch and the collision lower benchmark reduce to \(n^{-1}+d/n^2\) within the calibrated range. At \(\sigma=2\), the radius-indexed law class equals the unrestricted-radius class from \cref{obj:def:unrestricted-class}, and the upper expression is the polynomial large-alphabet rate.

The final result in this appendix localizes the regimes in which the finite-range bracket is order matched. It uses the baseline \(b_{n,d}\), the polynomial scale \(u_{n,d}\), and the selector and converse benchmarks \(q_{n,d,\sigma}\) and \(\ell_{n,d,\sigma}\).

\begin{theoremv}{thm:fixed-interior-tightness-and-shrinking-radius-gap}[Fixed-interior gap characterization]
For every overlap parameter \(\epsilon\) with \(0<\epsilon<1/2\), there is a constant \(c_\epsilon>0\) such that the following statements hold.  Write
\[
L_n:=\log(en),\qquad
b_{n,d}:=\frac1n+\frac d{n^2},\qquad
u_{n,d}:=\frac{d^2}{n^2L_n^2},
\]
and
\[
q_{n,d,\sigma}:=\frac1n+\min\left\{u_{n,d},\,\sigma^2+\frac d{n^2}\right\},
\qquad
\ell_{n,d,\sigma}:=b_{n,d}+\sigma^2u_{n,d}.
\]
Also let \(r_{n,d,\sigma}\) denote the frontier rate
\[
r_{n,d,\sigma}:=\frac1n+\min\left\{1,\,\min\{u_{n,d},\,\left(\sigma^2+\frac d{n^2}\right)\}\right\},
\]
and let \(\mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\) be the minimax mean-squared risk in \cref{obj:def:minimax-risk}.

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Triangular benchmark algebra.)}  For all \(n,d\in\mathbb N\) and all \(\sigma\in[0,2]\), if
\[
L_n^2<d<nL_n,
\]
then
\[
\frac12\left\{b_{n,d}+\min(u_{n,d},\sigma^2)\right\}
\le q_{n,d,\sigma}
\le b_{n,d}+\min(u_{n,d},\sigma^2),
\qquad
\ell_{n,d,\sigma}=b_{n,d}+\sigma^2u_{n,d}.
\]

\item \textbf{(Fixed interior radii.)}  For every \(\underline\sigma\in(0,2]\), there are constants \(c,C\) with \(0<c\le C\) such that, for all \(n,d\in\mathbb N\) and \(M,\sigma\in\mathbb R\), if
\[
n>0,\qquad d>0,\qquad M\ge1,\qquad
\underline\sigma\le\sigma\le2,\qquad
d\le c_\epsilon\frac{n^2}{L_n},
\]
then
\[
cM^2r_{n,d,\sigma}
\le
\mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}
\le
CM^2r_{n,d,\sigma}.
\]

\item \textbf{(Matching elbows.)}  For every \(K\ge0\), there are constants \(c,C\) with \(0<c\le C\) such that, for all \(n,d\in\mathbb N\) and \(M,\sigma\in\mathbb R\), if
\[
n>0,\qquad d>0,\qquad M\ge1,\qquad 0\le\sigma\le2,
\]
and at least one of
\[
u_{n,d}\ge1,\qquad
u_{n,d}\le Kb_{n,d},\qquad
\sigma^2\le Kb_{n,d}
\]
holds, then
\[
cM^2r_{n,d,\sigma}
\le
\mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}
\le
CM^2r_{n,d,\sigma}.
\]

\item \textbf{(Exact algebraic elbow.)}  For all \(n,d\in\mathbb N\) with \(n>0\),
\[
u_{n,d}\le b_{n,d}
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
d^2\le nL_n^2+dL_n^2.
\]

\item \textbf{(Residual wedge implication.)}  For every alphabet sequence \(d_t\) and radius sequence \(\sigma_t\), if eventually
\[
d_t\ge1,\qquad 0\le\sigma_t\le2,
\]
and
\[
\frac{r_{t,d_t,\sigma_t}}{t^{-1}+\min\{1,d_t/t^2\}+\sigma_t^2\min\{1,u_{t,d_t}\}}\longrightarrow\infty,
\]
then the sequence lies in the residual wedge:
\[
\frac{b_{t,d_t}}{u_{t,d_t}}\longrightarrow0,\qquad
u_{t,d_t}\longrightarrow0,\qquad
\frac{b_{t,d_t}}{\sigma_t^2}\longrightarrow0,\qquad
\sigma_t^2\longrightarrow0.
\]

\item \textbf{(Witness sequence.)}  The sequence \(d_n=n\) and \(\sigma_n=L_n^{-1/2}\) lies in the residual wedge.  Along this sequence,
\[
\frac{q_{n,n,L_n^{-1/2}}}{\ell_{n,n,L_n^{-1/2}}}
\asymp L_n,
\qquad
\frac{q_{n,n,L_n^{-1/2}}}{\ell_{n,n,L_n^{-1/2}}}\longrightarrow\infty .
\]
\end{itemize}
\end{theoremv}

\Cref{obj:thm:fixed-interior-tightness-and-shrinking-radius-gap} gives the finite-range phase algebra supporting the matched-regime discussion in the main text. For every radius bounded below by a positive constant, the minimax risk is comparable to \(M^2r_{n,d,\sigma}\) over the calibrated alphabet range. The same comparison holds at the saturation and parametric-dominance elbows. In the triangular region \(L_n^2<d<nL_n\), the theorem separates the selector expression \(b_{n,d}+\min(u_{n,d},\sigma^2)\) from the converse expression \(b_{n,d}+\sigma^2u_{n,d}\), and the witness sequence shows that the residual shrinking-radius region has logarithmic benchmark separation.


\section{Verification note}

This appendix records the scope of the machine-checked mathematical layer supporting the paper. The formalization covers the finite-cell observed-data model, the radius-indexed real-outcome classes, the estimators, the embeddings used for lower bounds, the selector risk bound, the endpoint reductions, the all-alphabet minimax bracket, and the regime-localization algebra. The displayed binary source lemmas are checked declarations in the current artifact; \citet{ZengBalakrishnanHanKennedy2024} supplies their statistical origin and attribution. Other citations supply source motivation and statistical context.

The setup objects are represented by \cref{obj:ass:consistency,obj:ass:conditional-exchangeability,obj:ass:overlap,obj:ass:mean-normalization,obj:ass:approximate-homogeneity,obj:ass:second-central-moment}, together with the model, experiment, target, and risk definitions in \cref{obj:def:model-class,obj:def:unrestricted-class,obj:def:sample-experiment,obj:def:ate-functional,obj:def:minimax-risk}. The identification and scale-normalization steps are represented by \cref{obj:prop:ate-identification,obj:lem:scale-sanity}. These statements fix the statistical experiment whose minimax risk is studied throughout the paper.

The constructive side is represented by the estimator definitions in \cref{obj:def:polynomial-handle,obj:def:collision-handle,obj:def:total-estimator} and by the risk guarantees in \cref{obj:thm:robust-upper-construction-resolution-all-d,obj:thm:frontier-upper-all-d}. The lower-bound side is represented by the embedding and least-favorable constructions in \cref{obj:def:least-favorable-handle,obj:synth_4,obj:synth_7}, the binary-to-real transfer in \cref{obj:prop:zeng-class-inclusion-and-lower-transfer}, the exact-homogeneity lower transfer in \cref{obj:lem:scaled-binary-exact-lower-transfer-all-d}, and the radius-channel converse in \cref{obj:thm:radius-channel-converse-all-d}. The binary source lemmas in \cref{obj:lem:binary-exact-collision-lower,obj:lem:zeng-binary-exact-homogeneity-lower,obj:lem:zeng-binary-one-arm-lower,obj:lem:zeng-usable-occupancy-reciprocal} are checked declarations with faithful proof audits in the verification contract, while \citet{ZengBalakrishnanHanKennedy2024} supplies their origin and statistical context.

The paper's main synthesis is represented by \cref{obj:thm:two-sided-minimax-bracket-all-d,obj:prop:endpoint-reductions-all-d,obj:thm:fixed-interior-tightness-and-shrinking-radius-gap-all-d}. These statements assemble the all-alphabet bracket, identify the endpoint reductions, and characterize the fixed-radius, saturation, parametric-dominance, and residual shrinking-radius regimes. The auxiliary binary collision comparison in \cref{obj:thm:published-binary-collision-comparison} is machine-checked conditionally on the displayed published guarantee. The open regime description in \cref{obj:oeq:shrinking-radius-frontier} is part of the recorded statement layer.

The anchored theorem, proposition, lemma, definition, and assumption statements displayed in the paper are machine-checked in Lean 4 at verification contract commit \texttt{d110bd64f2651b9ae5401ca84bbb269b5a579546}. The main bracket in \cref{obj:thm:two-sided-minimax-bracket-all-d}, selector upper bound in \cref{obj:thm:frontier-upper-all-d}, radius-channel converse in \cref{obj:thm:radius-channel-converse-all-d}, endpoint reductions in \cref{obj:prop:endpoint-reductions-all-d}, and regime theorem in \cref{obj:thm:fixed-interior-tightness-and-shrinking-radius-gap-all-d} are checked statements with faithful proof audits. The auxiliary comparison in \cref{obj:thm:published-binary-collision-comparison} is checked conditionally on the published binary collision guarantee displayed in its statement. Citations otherwise provide attribution and statistical context. The Lean toolchain is pinned by \texttt{lean-toolchain} to \texttt{leanprover/lean4:v4.33.0}; the repository build is invoked with \texttt{lake -d CausalSmith build}. The declaration names, source files, and paper object labels are recorded in \texttt{verification\_contract.json} and \texttt{presentation\_crosswalk.json} in the bundle.


\section{Proofs of the main results}\label{sec:deferred-proofs}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:prop:ate-identification}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item
For each cell \(k=1,\ldots,d\), set
\[
p_k=P(X=k);
\]
when \(d=0\), all sums below are empty.  When \(p_k>0\), let \(\pi_k=P(A=1\mid X=k)\).  The finite-cell law carries an arm-cell outcome law \(\nu_{ak}\) for every arm \(a\in\{0,1\}\) and every cell, including cells with \(p_k=0\), and its totalized mean is
\[
\mu_{ak}:=\int y\,d\nu_{ak}(y).
\]
For every supported cell and every Borel set \(\mathfrak A\subseteq\mathbb R\), the observed arm-cell factorization is
\[
P_O(X=k,A=a,Y\in\mathfrak A)
=
p_k
\begin{cases}
\pi_k, & a=1,\\
1-\pi_k, & a=0
\end{cases}
\nu_{ak}(\mathfrak A).
\]
If \(p_k>0\), \cref{obj:ass:overlap} and \(\epsilon>0\) make the displayed scalar positive for both arms.  Hence, on supported cells, \(\nu_{ak}\) is the observed conditional law of \(Y\) given \((A=a,X=k)\), and \(\mu_{ak}\) is the corresponding observed conditional mean.  If \(p_k=0\), then \(P_O(X=k)=0\); the totalized law \(\nu_{ak}\) and mean \(\mu_{ak}\) enter the finite formula below only through terms multiplied by \(p_k\). % lean: observed_arm_outcome_measure

\item
Fix \(a\in\{0,1\}\) and a supported cell \(k\), so \(p_k>0\).  By \cref{obj:ass:consistency}, on \(\{X=k,A=a\}\),
\[
Y(a)=Y
\quad\text{almost surely}.
\]
Together with the observed arm-cell factorization, this gives, for every Borel \(\mathfrak A\),
\[
P(X=k,A=a,Y(a)\in\mathfrak A)
=
p_k
\begin{cases}
\pi_k, & a=1,\\
1-\pi_k, & a=0
\end{cases}
\nu_{ak}(\mathfrak A).
\]
Applying \cref{obj:ass:conditional-exchangeability} in cell \(k\) to the potential-outcome event \(\{Y(a)\in\mathfrak A\}\) yields
\[
P(X=k,A=a,Y(a)\in\mathfrak A)\,P(X=k)
=
P(X=k,A=a)\,P(X=k,Y(a)\in\mathfrak A).
\]
Here \(P(X=k)=p_k\), and \(P(X=k,A=a)\) is \(p_k\pi_k\) when \(a=1\) and \(p_k(1-\pi_k)\) when \(a=0\).  The same overlap argument makes this arm-cell probability positive, so cancellation gives
\[
P(X=k,Y(a)\in\mathfrak A)=p_k\nu_{ak}(\mathfrak A).
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\mathcal L_P(Y(a);\,X=k)=p_k\,\nu_{ak},
\]
where the left side is the pushforward of the full-data law restricted to \(\{X=k\}\). % lean: full_cell_potential_measure

\item
The preceding restricted-law identity supplies the full-data integrability.  On supported cells, \cref{obj:ass:second-central-moment} gives integrability of \(y\mapsto (y-\mu_{ak})^2\) under \(\nu_{ak}\).  Thus \(y-\mu_{ak}\) is square-integrable, hence integrable, and adding the finite constant \(\mu_{ak}\) gives first-moment integrability of \(y\mapsto y\) under \(\nu_{ak}\).  The restricted-law identity transfers this integrability to \(Y(a)\) on each supported cell.  If \(p_k=0\), the full-data mass of \(\{X=k\}\) is \(0\), so the corresponding restricted measure is the zero measure.  Since there are finitely many cells,
\[
Y(1)\in L^1(P),\qquad Y(0)\in L^1(P).
\]
% lean: full_potential_integrable

\item
For every \(a\in\{0,1\}\) and every cell \(k\),
\[
\int_{\{X=k\}}Y(a)\,dP
=
p_k\int y\,d\nu_{ak}(y)
=
p_k\mu_{ak}.
\]
On supported cells this is the integral form of the restricted-law identity in the second step and the defining integral for \(\mu_{ak}\).  On zero-mass cells, the restricted measure is zero and \(p_k=0\), so both sides vanish. % lean: full_potential_cell_integral

\item
Apply the finite-fiber decomposition of the integral to the measurable finite-valued map \(X\).  Using the integrability just established,
\[
\int Y(1)\,dP
=
\sum_{k=1}^{d}\int_{\{X=k\}}Y(1)\,dP,
\qquad
\int Y(0)\,dP
=
\sum_{k=1}^{d}\int_{\{X=k\}}Y(0)\,dP.
\]
Therefore
\[
\begin{aligned}
\int\{Y(1)-Y(0)\}\,dP
&=
\sum_{k=1}^{d}
\left\{
\int_{\{X=k\}}Y(1)\,dP
-
\int_{\{X=k\}}Y(0)\,dP
\right\}\\
&=
\sum_{k=1}^{d}p_k(\mu_{1k}-\mu_{0k}).
\end{aligned}
\]
By \cref{obj:def:ate-functional},
\[
\tau(P)=\sum_{k=1}^{d}p_k(\mu_{1k}-\mu_{0k}),
\]
so
\[
\tau(P)
=
\int\{Y(1)-Y(0)\}\,dP.
\]
On supported cells, the first step identifies \(\mu_{ak}\) with \(E_P[Y\mid A=a,X=k]\); cells with \(p_k=0\) contribute \(0\) to the totalized sum.  Thus, when conditional expectations on zero-mass arm-cell events are chosen to equal the totalized means \(\mu_{ak}\), the same equality is written as
\[
\tau(P)
=
\int\{Y(1)-Y(0)\}\,dP
=
\sum_{k=1}^{d}p_k
\left\{
E_P[Y\mid A=1,X=k]-E_P[Y\mid A=0,X=k]
\right\}.
\]
The observed margin determines this finite-cell formula: \(p_k=P_O(X=k)\), and on every supported cell \cref{obj:ass:overlap} gives positive observed mass in both arms, so the observed conditional laws determine the two arm-cell means.  Zero-mass cells have weight \(p_k=0\). % lean: ate_identification
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:scale-sanity}]
Fix \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M}^{\mathrm{unr}}\).  Since \(M\ge1\), we have \(M\ge0\).  For every supported cell \(k\),
\[
|\tau_k(P)|
=
|\mu_{1k}(P)-\mu_{0k}(P)|
\le |\mu_{1k}(P)|+|\mu_{0k}(P)|
\le M/2+M/2
=M .
\]
% lean: scale_sanity
For every cell \(k\), the product term in the all-cell formula of \cref{obj:def:ate-functional} satisfies
\[
|p_k(P)\tau_k(P)|\le p_k(P)M .
\]
Indeed, the cell-mass range gives either \(p_k(P)=0\) or \(p_k(P)>0\).  In the first case both sides are zero.  In the second case,
\[
|p_k(P)\tau_k(P)|
=p_k(P)|\tau_k(P)|
\le p_k(P)M,
\]
using the supported-cell bound above and \(p_k(P)>0\). % lean: scale_sanity
The finite cell masses satisfy
\[
\sum_{k\in[d]}p_k(P)\le1 .
\]
% lean: sum_cellMass_le_one
Hence, using the all-cell formula of \cref{obj:def:ate-functional},
\[
|\tau(P)|
=
\left|\sum_{k\in[d]}p_k(P)\tau_k(P)\right|
\le
\sum_{k\in[d]}|p_k(P)\tau_k(P)|
\le
\sum_{k\in[d]}p_k(P)M
=
\left(\sum_{k\in[d]}p_k(P)\right)M
\le M .
\]
% lean: scale_sanity
Therefore, for every supported cell \(k\),
\[
|\delta_k(P)|
=
|\tau_k(P)-\tau(P)|
\le |\tau_k(P)|+|\tau(P)|
\le M+M
=
2M .
\]
% lean: scale_sanity

The radius-two membership follows by keeping the same law and the same consistency, exchangeability, overlap, mean-normalization, and second-moment witnesses, using the numerical facts \(0\le2\le2\), and adding the just-proved supported-cell bound
\[
|\delta_k(P)|\le 2M=2\cdot M
\qquad (p_k(P)>0)
\]
as the approximate-homogeneity witness. % lean: scale_sanity
Thus every law in \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M}^{\mathrm{unr}}\) is represented in \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,2}\).  Conversely, a member of \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,2}\) carries all fields required for \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M}^{\mathrm{unr}}\); forgetting the homogeneity and radius fields leaves the same law. % lean: scale_sanity
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:thm:frontier-upper}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item Fix an overlap parameter \(\epsilon\) with \(0<\epsilon<1/2\).  By
\cref{obj:thm:robust-upper-construction-resolution-all-d}, choose
\(C_{\epsilon}^{0}>0\) and a polynomial handle
\((N_{\epsilon},\rho_{\epsilon})\), with \(\rho_{\epsilon}>0\), such that
\[
N_{\epsilon}\le n,
\qquad
 d\le \rho_{\epsilon}n\log(en)
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
2\le K_n
\]
for all positive integers \(n,d\).  The same construction gives, for every
\(n,d\ge1\), every \(M\ge1\), and every \(0\le\sigma\le2\), measurable
\([-M,M]\)-valued estimators \(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}\) and
\(T_n^{\mathrm{col}}\), and for every
\(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\),
\[
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
\left(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}-\tau(P)\right)^2
\right]
\le
C_{\epsilon}^{0}M^2
\left\{
\frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right)
\right\}
\]
and
\[
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
\left(T_n^{\mathrm{col}}-\tau(P)\right)^2
\right]
\le
C_{\epsilon}^{0}M^2
\left\{
\frac1n+\sigma^2+\frac{d}{n^2}
\right\}.
\]
Set
\[
C_{\epsilon}:=\max\{1,C_{\epsilon}^{0}\}.
\]
Then \(C_{\epsilon}>0\) and \(C_{\epsilon}\ge C_{\epsilon}^{0}\), so both
displayed risk bounds hold with \(C_{\epsilon}\) in place of
\(C_{\epsilon}^{0}\). % lean: robust_upper_construction_resolution_all_d

\item For fixed admissible \(n,d,M,\sigma\), put
\[
u_{n,d}:=\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)},
\qquad
h_{n,d,\sigma}:=\sigma^2+\frac{d}{n^2},
\qquad
r_{n,d,\sigma}:=\frac1n+\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d},h_{n,d,\sigma}\}\}.
\]
The known-radius selector of \cref{obj:def:total-estimator} is the three-branch
estimator
\[
\widehat\tau_n^{\star}
=
\begin{cases}
T_n^{\mathrm{col}},
& h_{n,d,\sigma}\le \min\{1,u_{n,d}\},\\
T_n^{\mathrm{poly}},
& h_{n,d,\sigma}> \min\{1,u_{n,d}\}
  \text{ and } u_{n,d}<\min\{1,h_{n,d,\sigma}\},\\
0,
& h_{n,d,\sigma}> \min\{1,u_{n,d}\}
  \text{ and } u_{n,d}\ge \min\{1,h_{n,d,\sigma}\}.
\end{cases}
\]
The first two branches are elements of \(\mathcal T_{n,M}\) by the construction
above.  The zero branch is measurable and takes values in \([-M,M]\) because
\(M\ge1\).  Hence \(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}\in\mathcal T_{n,M}\). % lean: totalSelector

\item Fix \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\).  On the collision branch,
\(h_{n,d,\sigma}\le \min\{1,u_{n,d}\}\), so
\[
\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d},h_{n,d,\sigma}\}\}=h_{n,d,\sigma}.
\]
Therefore
\[
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
\left(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}-\tau(P)\right)^2
\right]
\le
C_{\epsilon}M^2\left(\frac1n+h_{n,d,\sigma}\right)
=
C_{\epsilon}M^2 r_{n,d,\sigma}.
\]
On the polynomial branch, \(u_{n,d}<\min\{1,h_{n,d,\sigma}\}\), so
\[
\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}=u_{n,d},
\qquad
\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d},h_{n,d,\sigma}\}\}=u_{n,d}.
\]
Thus
\[
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
\left(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}-\tau(P)\right)^2
\right]
\le
C_{\epsilon}M^2\left(\frac1n+u_{n,d}\right)
=
C_{\epsilon}M^2 r_{n,d,\sigma}.
\]
It remains to consider the zero branch.  There
\[
h_{n,d,\sigma}> \min\{1,u_{n,d}\},
\qquad
u_{n,d}\ge \min\{1,h_{n,d,\sigma}\}.
\]
These inequalities imply \(1\le u_{n,d}\): if \(u_{n,d}<1\), then
\(h_{n,d,\sigma}>u_{n,d}\), while
\(\min\{1,h_{n,d,\sigma}\}\le u_{n,d}\); if \(h_{n,d,\sigma}\le1\) this gives
\(h_{n,d,\sigma}\le u_{n,d}<h_{n,d,\sigma}\), and if \(h_{n,d,\sigma}>1\) it
gives \(1\le u_{n,d}<1\).  Hence \(1\le u_{n,d}\).  The first zero-branch
inequality then gives \(1<h_{n,d,\sigma}\), so \(1\le h_{n,d,\sigma}\), and
\[
\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d},h_{n,d,\sigma}\}\}=1.
\]
Viewing the same law as a member of the unrestricted-radius class, the scale
bound in \cref{obj:lem:scale-sanity} gives
\[
|\tau(P)|\le M.
\]
Since the selected estimator is identically zero on this branch,
\[
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
\left(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}-\tau(P)\right)^2
\right]
=
\tau(P)^2
\le M^2
\le C_{\epsilon}M^2\left(\frac1n+1\right)
=
C_{\epsilon}M^2 r_{n,d,\sigma},
\]
where the penultimate inequality uses \(C_{\epsilon}\ge1\) and \(n\ge1\).
Together the three branches prove the asserted risk bound for every
\(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\). % lean: frontier_upper_bound_all_d

\item Put \(c_{\epsilon}:=1\).  Then \(c_{\epsilon}>0\).  The preceding
all-alphabet bound applies to every positive \(n,d\), so it applies in
particular under the restricted-dimensional hypothesis
\[
d\le c_{\epsilon}\frac{n^2}{\log(en)}.
\]
The constants \(C_{\epsilon},c_{\epsilon}\) and the same polynomial handle
\((N_{\epsilon},\rho_{\epsilon})\) therefore satisfy all claims of the theorem.
% lean: frontier_upper
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:prop:endpoint-reductions}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item First record the logarithmic estimate used below.  If \(n\ge1\), then
\[
L_n=\log(en)=1+\log n .
\]
The standard bound \(\log n\le n^{1/2}/(1/2)=2\sqrt n\), together with
\(\log n\ge0\) and \(\sqrt n\ge1\), gives
\[
0\le L_n\le 1+2\sqrt n\le 3\sqrt n .
\]
Squaring yields
\[
L_n^2\le 9n .
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteAteHeterogeneityFrontier.logEN_sq_le_nine_mul

\item We prove the all-alphabet comparison that supplies the constants.  For \(n,d\ge1\), put
\[
\eta_{n,d}:=\frac{d}{n^2},
\qquad
u_{n,d}:=\frac{d^2}{n^2L_n^2}.
\]
Since \(L_n\ge1\), both \(\eta_{n,d}\) and \(u_{n,d}\) are nonnegative.  At radius two the collision term is
\[
2^2+\eta_{n,d}=4+\eta_{n,d}\ge1,
\]
and therefore
\[
\frac1n+\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d},4+\eta_{n,d}\}\}
=
\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}.
\]
At radius zero,
\[
\frac1n+\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d},\eta_{n,d}\}\}
\le
\frac1n+\min\{1,\eta_{n,d}\},
\]
and the capped converse expression at radius zero is exactly the right-hand side.

It remains to compare the radius-two converse term
\[
\frac1n+\min\{1,\eta_{n,d}\}+4\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}
\]
with
\[
\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}.
\]
If \(L_n^2\le d\), then
\[
\eta_{n,d}=\frac d{n^2}\le \frac{d^2}{n^2L_n^2}=u_{n,d},
\]
so
\[
\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d},\eta_{n,d}\}\}=\min\{1,\eta_{n,d}\}
\]
and
\[
\min\{1,\eta_{n,d}\}\le \min\{1,u_{n,d}\}.
\]
Consequently
\[
\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}
\le
\frac1n+\min\{1,\eta_{n,d}\}+4\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}
\le
5\left(\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}\right).
\]
If \(d<L_n^2\), the logarithmic estimate from the previous step gives
\[
d\le 9n,
\qquad
\eta_{n,d}\le \frac9n .
\]
Hence
\[
\frac1n+\min\{1,\eta_{n,d}\}\le \frac{10}{n}.
\]
The upper-frontier term at radius zero is at least \(1/n\), so
\[
\frac1{10}\left(\frac1n+\min\{1,\eta_{n,d}\}\right)
\le
\frac1n+\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d},\eta_{n,d}\}\}.
\]
The remaining upper bound at radius zero follows from the displayed monotonicity inequality.  For radius two, nonnegativity gives the lower bound
\[
\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}
\le
\frac1n+\min\{1,\eta_{n,d}\}+4\min\{1,u_{n,d}\},
\]
while \(\min\{1,\eta_{n,d}\}\le 9/n\) gives
\[
\frac1n+\min\{1,\eta_{n,d}\}+4\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}
\le
10\left(\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}\right).
\]
Thus, uniformly over all \(n,d\ge1\), the all-alphabet comparisons hold with
\[
c=\frac1{10},
\qquad
C=10 .
\]
The radius-two equality of attainable laws is exactly the consequence of \cref{obj:lem:scale-sanity}.
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteAteHeterogeneityFrontier.endpoint_reductions_all_d

\item Now fix \(\epsilon\in(0,1/2)\) and choose
\[
c_\epsilon=1,
\qquad
c=\frac1{10},
\qquad
C=10 .
\]
Then \(0<c_\epsilon\le1\) and \(0<c\le C\).  Suppose \(n,d\ge1\) and
\[
d\le c_\epsilon\frac{n^2}{L_n}=\frac{n^2}{L_n}.
\]
Because \(L_n\ge1\),
\[
d\le n^2,
\qquad
\frac d{n^2}\le1,
\qquad
\min\left\{1,\frac d{n^2}\right\}=\frac d{n^2}.
\]
Applying the all-alphabet comparison from the previous step and replacing the capped term by \(d/n^2\) gives
\[
c\left(\frac1n+\frac d{n^2}\right)
\le
\frac1n+\min\left\{1,\min\left\{u_{n,d},\frac d{n^2}\right\}\right\}
\le
C\left(\frac1n+\frac d{n^2}\right),
\]
and
\[
c\left(\frac1n+\frac d{n^2}\right)
\le
\frac1n+\min\left\{1,\frac d{n^2}\right\}
\le
C\left(\frac1n+\frac d{n^2}\right).
\]
The class identity is the radius-two conclusion of \cref{obj:lem:scale-sanity}.  Finally, the all-alphabet radius-two calculation gives, for every \(n,d\ge1\),
\[
\frac1n+\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d},4+d/n^2\}\}
=
\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\},
\]
and its converse comparison gives
\[
c\left(\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}\right)
\le
\frac1n+\min\left\{1,\frac d{n^2}\right\}+4\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}
\le
C\left(\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}\right).
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteAteHeterogeneityFrontier.endpoint_reductions
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:thm:radius-channel-converse}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item Fix an overlap parameter \(\epsilon\) with \(0<\epsilon<1/2\).  Apply \cref{obj:thm:radius-channel-converse-all-d} to obtain constants \(\bar c_{\epsilon}\) and \(\bar b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}\) such that
\[
0<\bar c_{\epsilon}\le 1,
\qquad
0<\bar b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}},
\]
and, for every admissible \(n,d,M,\sigma\),
\[
\bar c_{\epsilon}M^2
\left\{
\frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac d{n^2}\right)
+\sigma^2
\min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right)
\right\}
\le
\mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}.
\]
Set \(c_{\epsilon}:=\bar c_{\epsilon}\) and
\(b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}:=\bar b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}\). % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteAteHeterogeneityFrontier.radius_channel_converse_all_d

\item Now fix \(n,d,M,\sigma\) satisfying the hypotheses of the present statement, and put
\[
L:=\log(en).
\]
Since \(0<n\), we have \(n\ge1\), hence
\[
L=\log(e n)=1+\log n\ge1.
\]
The restricted range and \(c_{\epsilon}\le1\) give
\[
d\le c_{\epsilon}\frac{n^2}{L}
\le c_{\epsilon}n^2
\le n^2 .
\]
Because \(n>0\), division by \(n^2\) yields
\[
\frac d{n^2}\le1,
\qquad\text{so}\qquad
\min\left(1,\frac d{n^2}\right)=\frac d{n^2}.
\]
Substituting this identity into the all-alphabet bound displayed above gives
\[
\mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}
\ge
c_{\epsilon}M^2
\left\{
\frac1n+\frac d{n^2}
+\sigma^2
\min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right)
\right\}.
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteAteHeterogeneityFrontier.radius_channel_converse

\item The radial certificate supplied by \cref{obj:thm:radius-channel-converse-all-d} uses the same constants \(c_{\epsilon}\) and \(b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}\).  Unpacking the certificate, there are an integer cap \(d_{\mathrm{rad}}\), a binary control-zero source class \(\mathcal S_{\mathrm{rad}}\), a padding map into the \(d\)-alphabet, an embedding \(\Psi\) that sends each source member into \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\), and binary full-observation couplings such that the channel acts by
\[
B'(a)\mid B(a)
\sim
\operatorname{Bernoulli}\!\left\{
\frac12+\frac{\sigma}{2}\left(B(a)-\frac12\right)
\right\},
\qquad
Y(a):=M\{B'(a)-1/2\}.
\]
The source target in this certificate is the total treated-arm weighted functional
\[
\psi(P)
:=
\sum_{k=1}^{d_{\mathrm{rad}}}
p_k(P)\,\mu_{1k}^{\mathrm{bin}}(P),
\]
where \(\mu_{1k}^{\mathrm{bin}}(P)\) is the treated-arm binary mean coordinate carried by the source law in cell \(k\), defined for every source cell including zero-mass cells.  Thus, for all \(P_0,P_1\in\mathcal S_{\mathrm{rad}}\), the same certificate gives
\[
\tau(\Psi(P_1))-\tau(\Psi(P_0))
=
M\frac{\sigma}{2}
\left\{
\psi(P_1)-\psi(P_0)
\right\}.
\]
It also gives the estimator-wise lower bound: for every
\(\widehat\tau\in\mathcal T_{n,M}\), the class of total measurable \([-M,M]\)-valued estimators from \cref{obj:def:minimax-risk}, there is \(P\in\mathcal S_{\mathrm{rad}}\) such that
\[
c_{\epsilon}M^2\sigma^2
\min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right)
\le
\mathbb E_{\Psi(P)}
\left[
\{\widehat\tau-\tau(\Psi(P))\}^2
\right].
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteAteHeterogeneityFrontier.radius_channel_converse
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:thm:robust-upper-construction-resolution}]
Fix an overlap parameter \(\epsilon\) with \(0<\epsilon<1/2\).

\begin{enumerate}
\item First form the all-alphabet package.  By
\cref{obj:lem:continuous-ratio-polynomial-upper-all-d}, there are
\(C_{\mathrm{poly},\epsilon}>0\) and a polynomial handle
\[
\mathfrak h_\epsilon=(N_{\mathrm{cal},\epsilon},\rho_{\mathrm{cal},\epsilon}),
\qquad \rho_{\mathrm{cal},\epsilon}>0,
\]
such that, for all integers \(n,d\ge1\),
\[
N_{\mathrm{cal},\epsilon}\le n,\qquad
d\le \rho_{\mathrm{cal},\epsilon}n\log(en)
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
2\le K_n,
\]
and, for every \(M\ge1\), \(0\le\sigma\le2\), and
\(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\),
\[
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
\left(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}-\tau(P)\right)^2
\right]
\le
C_{\mathrm{poly},\epsilon}M^2
\left\{
\frac1n+
\min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right)
\right\}.
\]
The same cited construction gives measurability of \(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}\) and
\[
T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}(s)\in[-M,M]
\qquad\text{for every }s\in\mathcal O_d^n .
\]

By \cref{obj:lem:continuous-occupancy-collision-upper-all-d}, there is
\(C_{\mathrm{col},\epsilon}>0\) such that, for every \(n,d\ge1\),
\(M\ge1\), \(0\le\sigma\le2\), and
\(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\),
\[
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
\left(T_n^{\mathrm{col}}-\tau(P)\right)^2
\right]
\le
C_{\mathrm{col},\epsilon}M^2
\left(
\frac1n+\sigma^2+\frac{d}{n^2}
\right).
\]
For the admissibility of the collision estimator, write its unclipped inner
statistic as the total sample function
\[
\Gamma_{\mathrm{col}}(s)
:=
\begin{cases}
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{R_n(s)}
\sum_{k=1}^d
N_k(s)I_k(s)
\left[
\mathbf 1\{N_{1k}(s)>0\}\frac{S_{1k}(s)}{N_{1k}(s)\vee 1}
-
\mathbf 1\{N_{0k}(s)>0\}\frac{S_{0k}(s)}{N_{0k}(s)\vee 1}
\right],
& R_n(s)>0,\\[1.2em]
0,& R_n(s)=0.
\end{cases}
\]
Here \(m\vee1\) denotes \(\max\{m,1\}\).  The displayed arm factors are total
functions of the sample and agree with the arm-cell means of
\cref{obj:def:collision-handle} on positive arm-cell counts; multiplication by
\(I_k\) selects exactly the cells where both arm-cell counts are positive.  Hence
\cref{obj:def:collision-handle} gives
\[
T_n^{\mathrm{col}}(s)=\max\{-M,\min(M,\Gamma_{\mathrm{col}}(s))\}.
\]
The counts, indicators, outcome totals, finite sums, totalized ratios, and the
fallback at \(R_n=0\) are measurable functions of the sample, so
\(T_n^{\mathrm{col}}\) is measurable.  Since \(M\ge1\), clipping gives
\[
T_n^{\mathrm{col}}(s)\in[-M,M]
\qquad\text{for every }s\in\mathcal O_d^n .
\]

Set
\[
C_\epsilon:=\max\{C_{\mathrm{poly},\epsilon},
                 C_{\mathrm{col},\epsilon}\}.
\]
Then \(C_\epsilon>0\).  The two displayed rate factors are nonnegative for
\(n,d\ge1\) and \(0\le\sigma\le2\), and \(M^2\ge0\); hence replacing
\(C_{\mathrm{poly},\epsilon}\) and \(C_{\mathrm{col},\epsilon}\) by
\(C_\epsilon\) preserves the two upper bounds. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteAteHeterogeneityFrontier.robust_upper_construction_resolution_all_d

\item Define
\[
c_\epsilon:=1.
\]
Then \(c_\epsilon>0\).  For arbitrary \(n,d\ge1\), \(M\ge1\), and
\(0\le\sigma\le2\), the all-alphabet package from the first step gives the
measurability and range assertions for both estimators.  If
\[
d\le c_\epsilon\frac{n^2}{\log(en)},
\]
then for every \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\) the same all-alphabet
risk bounds apply under this range condition:
\[
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
\left(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}-\tau(P)\right)^2
\right]
\le
C_\epsilon M^2
\left\{
\frac1n+
\min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right)
\right\},
\]
and
\[
E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[
\left(T_n^{\mathrm{col}}-\tau(P)\right)^2
\right]
\le
C_\epsilon M^2
\left(
\frac1n+\sigma^2+\frac{d}{n^2}
\right).
\]
Together with the active-range property of \(\mathfrak h_\epsilon\), these are
the asserted constants and calibrated handle. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteAteHeterogeneityFrontier.robust_upper_construction_resolution
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:thm:fixed-interior-tightness-and-shrinking-radius-gap}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item Fix \(0<\epsilon<1/2\), and apply \cref{obj:thm:fixed-interior-tightness-and-shrinking-radius-gap-all-d} at this \(\epsilon\).  Choose the range constant
\[
c_\epsilon:=1 .
\]
Then \(c_\epsilon>0\).  The triangular benchmark algebra, the exact algebraic elbow, the residual wedge implication, and the diagonal witness assertions in the present statement are exactly the corresponding conclusions supplied by \cref{obj:thm:fixed-interior-tightness-and-shrinking-radius-gap-all-d}, with the same definitions of \(L_n\), \(b_{n,d}\), \(u_{n,d}\), \(q_{n,d,\sigma}\), \(\ell_{n,d,\sigma}\), and \(r_{n,d,\sigma}\).  In particular, the denominator in the residual-wedge ratio is the capped converse benchmark
\[
\frac1t+\min\left\{1,\frac{d_t}{t^2}\right\}
+\sigma_t^2\min\{1,u_{t,d_t}\},
\]
so the all-alphabet residual-wedge implication gives precisely
\[
\frac{b_{t,d_t}}{u_{t,d_t}}\to0,\qquad
u_{t,d_t}\to0,\qquad
\frac{b_{t,d_t}}{\sigma_t^2}\to0,\qquad
\sigma_t^2\to0 .
\]
For the witness \(d_n=n\), \(\sigma_n=L_n^{-1/2}\), the same all-alphabet conclusion gives membership in the residual wedge and the two displayed comparisons
\[
\frac{q_{n,n,L_n^{-1/2}}}{\ell_{n,n,L_n^{-1/2}}}\asymp L_n,
\qquad
\frac{q_{n,n,L_n^{-1/2}}}{\ell_{n,n,L_n^{-1/2}}}\to\infty .
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteAteHeterogeneityFrontier.fixed_interior_tightness_and_shrinking_radius_gap_all_d

\item It remains only to specialize the fixed-interior and matching-elbow minimax clauses.  Let \(\underline\sigma\in(0,2]\).  The fixed-radius clause of \cref{obj:thm:fixed-interior-tightness-and-shrinking-radius-gap-all-d} supplies constants \(c,C\) with \(0<c\le C\) such that, for all \(n,d\in\mathbb N\), \(M,\sigma\in\mathbb R\),
\[
n>0,\qquad d>0,\qquad M\ge1,\qquad
0\le\sigma,\qquad \underline\sigma\le\sigma,\qquad \sigma\le2
\]
imply
\[
cM^2r_{n,d,\sigma}
\le
\mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}
\le
CM^2r_{n,d,\sigma}.
\]
Since the implication holds for every positive \(d\), it also holds under the additional displayed range condition
\[
d\le c_\epsilon\frac{n^2}{L_n}
=\frac{n^2}{L_n}.
\]
Thus the fixed-interior-radii conclusion follows with the same constants \(c,C\).  % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteAteHeterogeneityFrontier.fixed_interior_tightness_and_shrinking_radius_gap

\item Now fix \(K\ge0\).  The matching-elbow clause of \cref{obj:thm:fixed-interior-tightness-and-shrinking-radius-gap-all-d} supplies constants \(c,C\) with \(0<c\le C\) such that, whenever
\[
n>0,\qquad d>0,\qquad M\ge1,\qquad 0\le\sigma\le2
\]
and at least one of
\[
u_{n,d}\ge1,\qquad
u_{n,d}\le Kb_{n,d},\qquad
\sigma^2\le Kb_{n,d}
\]
holds, one has
\[
cM^2r_{n,d,\sigma}
\le
\mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}
\le
CM^2r_{n,d,\sigma}.
\]
This is exactly the matching-elbows assertion in the present theorem.  Combining this with the clauses recorded in the first step and the choice \(c_\epsilon=1\) proves all asserted conclusions.  % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteAteHeterogeneityFrontier.fixed_interior_tightness_and_shrinking_radius_gap
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:thm:two-sided-minimax-bracket}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item Fix an overlap parameter \(\epsilon\) with \(0<\epsilon<1/2\).  By \cref{obj:thm:two-sided-minimax-bracket-all-d}, choose constants \(c_{\epsilon}\) and \(C_{\epsilon}\) such that
\[
0<c_{\epsilon}\le 1,\qquad 1\le C_{\epsilon},\qquad c_{\epsilon}\le C_{\epsilon},
\]
and such that, for every \(n,d\ge1\), \(M\ge1\), and \(0\le\sigma\le2\),
\[
c_{\epsilon}M^2
\left\{
\frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac{d}{n^2}\right)
+\sigma^2\min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right)
\right\}
\le
\mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}
\]
and
\[
\mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}
\le
C_{\epsilon}M^2
\left\{
\frac1n+
\min\left(1,\min\left(\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)},\sigma^2+\frac{d}{n^2}\right)\right)
\right\}.
\]
These constants already have the normalization required in the statement. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteAteHeterogeneityFrontier.two_sided_minimax_bracket_all_d

\item Now fix \(n,d\ge1\), \(M\ge1\), and \(0\le\sigma\le2\), and assume
\[
d\le \frac{c_{\epsilon}n^2}{\log(en)} .
\]
Since \(n\ge1\),
\[
\log(en)=\log(e)+\log n=1+\log n\ge1.
\]
The numerator \(c_{\epsilon}n^2\) is nonnegative, so
\[
d\le \frac{c_{\epsilon}n^2}{\log(en)}
\le c_{\epsilon}n^2
\le n^2,
\]
where the last inequality uses \(c_{\epsilon}\le1\).  Dividing by \(n^2>0\) gives
\[
\frac{d}{n^2}\le1,
\qquad\text{and hence}\qquad
\min\left(1,\frac{d}{n^2}\right)=\frac{d}{n^2}.
\]
Substituting this identity into the lower bound from the preceding step yields
\[
c_{\epsilon}M^2\left\{
\frac{1}{n}+\frac{d}{n^2}
+\sigma^2\min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right)
\right\}
\le
\mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}.
\]
The upper bound is exactly the upper bound supplied in the preceding step.  Therefore the displayed lower and upper bounds hold throughout the stated restricted range. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteAteHeterogeneityFrontier.two_sided_minimax_bracket
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:scaled-binary-exact-lower-transfer-all-d}]
Fix \(0<\epsilon<1/2\).  Let \(a_\epsilon,b_\epsilon>0\) and
\(N_\epsilon\in\mathbb N\) be the constants supplied by
\cref{obj:lem:zeng-binary-exact-homogeneity-lower}.  Let
\(c_{\mathrm{par}}>0\) be the constant in \cref{obj:lem:parametric-lower}.
Set
\[
\bar b_\epsilon:=\min\{b_\epsilon,1/2\},
\qquad
K_\epsilon:=1+N_\epsilon+\frac1{\bar b_\epsilon},
\qquad
c_\epsilon:=\min\left\{\frac{a_\epsilon \bar b_\epsilon}{4},
\frac{c_{\mathrm{par}}}{K_\epsilon}\right\}.
\]
Then \(\bar b_\epsilon>0\), \(K_\epsilon>0\), and \(c_\epsilon>0\). % lean: scaled_binary_exact_lower_transfer_all_d

Fix \(n,d\ge1\), \(M\ge1\), and \(0\le\sigma\le2\).  Put
\[
x:=\bar b_\epsilon n^2 .
\]
First suppose that
\[
N_\epsilon\le n,
\qquad
1\le x .
\]
Define the capped source alphabet size
\[
d_0:=\min\{d,\lfloor x\rfloor\}.
\]
Then \(d_0\ge1\), \(d_0\le d\), and
\[
d_0\le b_\epsilon n^2 .
\] % lean: scaled_binary_exact_lower_transfer_all_d
The exact binary source bound gives
\[
a_\epsilon\left(\frac1n+\frac{d_0}{n^2}\right)
\le
\mathsf R^{\mathrm{bin}}_{n,d_0,\epsilon},
\]
